# MealKit changelog

Implementation details of finished roadmap items (what was built, why, how, and what's left). Future plans and order are in `docs/ROADMAP.md`, design answers in `docs/CODING_MODEL.md`. Reasons for decisions and features not included or deferred are in `docs/DECISIONS.md`.
When a roadmap item is done, its details move here and ROADMAP keeps only one line. Newest version on top.

## Editor UX pass (v0.9.x, 2026-11-04)

Review of 1600×900 and 1280×720 captures.
- **Value rows** (`FieldEditor.h`: `propertyRow`): names on the left in a shared column and the value filling the rest, in the inspector, environment, terrain/scatter, tuning, project settings, feedback and [UI] preview. ImGui's right-hand labels were clipped by the panel edge (`Jump force (m/`), since the width was measured from one fixed label. A name that doesn't fit gets `...` and its full text in a tooltip.
- **Color and noise**: `sectionHeader` draws sections in a neutral bar, so blue stays for selection and active states. Axis-colored bars on transform values, an aligned scale lock, a red hover on the inspector's Delete, no triangle on dock tab bars (`WindowMenuButtonPosition`).
- **Scene tree**: one row with **+ Add** (the [Add] menu's list; the four buttons overflowed the panel) and search. Kinds are dimmed after the name and shortened with `...`. A search without results says so, clicking empty space deselects.
- **Viewport toolbar**: three groups with separators that flow onto new rows when they don't fit beside the axis gizmo, so the toolbar no longer covers panels in a narrow viewport. The gizmo scales with DPI.
- **Menu bar**: player count, bots and shadow check are in one drop-down by the play button, hidden during play. The play timeline shrinks so **Save Scenario** stays on the bar at 1280 wide.
- **Menus**: every panel is under [View] (Assets, Log, Build Log and Performance moved from [Build]).
- **Messages** show once, as notices over the viewport (the status bar repeated them).
- **[UI] panel**: Penpot is a closed section at the bottom, edit help is a `(?)` tooltip.
- `--window WxH` sets the editor window size for checking layouts.

## Editor usability, first fixes (v0.9.x, 2026-11-04)

- **[Tables] showed `input.bindings` empty**: engine tables open before `setProject`, so they were read from a path with no project folder, and saving could have written there. `TablesPanel::setProjectDirectory` now sets the paths again and rereads open tables.
- **Table headers** show the field name only, in columns 110 to 200 wide. The tooltip has the name, description and field path. The panel note moved to a `(?)` tooltip.
- **Asset browser scan cache** (`EditorAssets.cpp`): folder listings and missing model checks run once a second instead of every frame, and right after a rename.
- `--tables` and `--tuning` bring those tabs to the front for captures.
- **Field attributes** (`FieldEditor.h`): the label takes `group`, `range`, `step`, `unit` and `pick` after the name. Sections are `sectionHeader`s; drags without `step` follow the size of the value. Cells in [Tables] use the same attributes.
- **Pickers**: `sound` and `effect` in `feedback.table` are searchable lists, and a value not in the list stays and shows in red. Drag and drop and a preview button aren't done.
- `samples/sandbox/sim/Tuning.h` uses the groups, ranges and units. Labels stay out of the content hash, but its simulation version changes because it hashes `sim/` sources. The saved replay still passes.

## Editor usability, gathering data (v0.9.x, 2026-11-04)

- **[Data] window** (`EditorData.cpp`): tuning, tables, feedback and sounds under one list with unsaved marks. It replaces the [Tuning] tab and the [Tables], [Feedback] and [Sounds] windows; those commands now open their page. It starts floating.
- **[Problems] window**: missing models, models over budget, and missing row references in tables, tuning and scene objects, checked twice a second. The count is on the tab. Clicking selects the object or opens the document. It replaces the list under the table.
- **Undo** (`UndoStack.h`): tuning, tables and feedback reactions keep text snapshots, one step per drag or typed value. Ctrl+Z and Ctrl+Y go to the open page while the [Data] window has focus, also during play. Deleting rows, reloading from file and restoring defaults can be undone, so they ask no confirmation. Effect files have no undo.

## Editor usability, selection and search (v0.9.x, 2026-11-04)

- **Command palette** (`FuzzyMatch.h`): letters in order with gaps, Korean initial consonants, best match first. Recent items lead when nothing is typed (kept for the session). It also finds assets, table rows and tuning values.
- **Scene tree badges**: `!` in red or orange with the reason in a tooltip, and a blue dot for fields changed from the kind's defaults. No prefab badge, because placed objects keep no link to their prefab.
- **Multi-object editing**: a field changed in the inspector goes to the other selected objects of the same kind. Fields that differ get a dash and a tinted value. Name and transform values still edit one object.

## Editor usability, tree folders and thumbnails (v0.9.x, 2026-11-04)

- **Scene tree folders**: `Entity::folder` is a path saved as a `folder` line (`.scene` format 5) and left out of the content hash, so it needs no folder objects and empty folders don't exist. Drag objects onto a folder, or use **Move to Folder...**; folders can be renamed or removed, with undo. Changing `scene/` and `core/` changes the simulation version of every game; simulation results don't change.
- **Asset grid** (`AssetThumbnails.h`): the [Assets] slider switches between the list and a grid. Models are drawn one per frame to small targets and redrawn when the file changes, up to 128 for the session. Nothing is cached on disk yet, and targets are not freed until the editor closes. Known issue: the first cell's name loses its first letter (`ring.glb` shows as `ing.glb`), cause not found; the tooltip has the full path.

## Editor usability, viewport handles and density (v0.9.x, 2026-11-04)

- **Viewport handles**: an f32 field labeled `gizmo=radius` draws three circles around the selected object and a handle that drags the value, as one undo step. The point light's range uses it. `gizmo=point` isn't done: nothing uses it yet.
- **Compact rows** (Preferences > Interface, `--compact` for captures): half the vertical padding and spacing.
- **Color tokens** (`EditorColors.h`): danger, warning, good, changed and play replace literal colors of those meanings in the main panels. Toolbar icons aren't done.

## Editor scene view tab (v0.9.x, 2026-11-04)

- **Scene view tab** (`Editor::drawSceneWindow`): the scene view is a dock window, so other windows can sit beside it as tabs and it can dock elsewhere. The 3D picture stays on the back buffer and fits the window's area, because offscreen targets have no shadows. It can't float: released outside a dock, it returns to the center. Behind another tab the gizmo, toolbar and picking stop, and starting play brings it to the front.
- **[View] menu** lists only [Data] for tuning, tables, feedback and sounds. Their commands stay in the palette and shortcuts.

## Game UI editor in the editor (v0.9.x)

- **Step 1, editable `.ui` source** (2026-11-04, `engine/libs/mkui/include/mkui/Source.h`): `parseSource`/`writeSource` keep a `.ui` file as written (comments, blank lines, property order, `$theme` tokens, `use` components and `set` lines) as a tree of nodes whose items are lines or child places, so a save changes only the lines an edit touched. `Document` can't be written back because it inlines components and resolves number tokens; the panel will edit the source, write it, and read it again with `parseDocument` (one parser). `mapDocumentNodes` maps each `Document` node to its source node (nodes inside a component map to the `use` node). Edits: `getProperty`/`setProperty`/`removeProperty` by leading words (`pos`, `on hover fill`, `set label color`, `span 1`; a new line goes after the node's last property line), `addNode`/`removeNode`/`moveNode` by child-index paths (the board can't move or go, a node can't move into itself). Penpot exports are flagged (`Source::exported`) for read-only opening.
- **Step 2, selecting and editing in the [UI] panel** (2026-11-04, `editor/UiPanelEdit.cpp`): **Edit screen > Edit** turns the panel into an editor of the open screen's source. The preview shows unsaved text through `ui::System::setDraft` (mkui `System::setDraft`: the screen is read from the given text instead of its file; `nullptr` goes back to the file), so edits appear at once with the real fonts, strings and view model values. Selection by clicking the scene view (deepest visible box under the mouse from `boxes()`, which now carry their `Document` node index; `Ctrl`+click steps to the box beneath, since `Alt`+click orbits the camera) or the node tree; the selection is outlined. Properties: name, position (hidden for nodes a flex/grid parent places), size, fill/text color with widgets, then every line of the node as editable text, **x** to remove, **Add...** with the property list (`on`/`set`/`span`/`shadow` lines are added, others replace). History is the panel's own (text snapshots committed when no widget is active, so a drag or typing is one step); while the panel edits, the editor's Undo/Redo/Delete commands go to it, and Save (`Ctrl+S`) also writes the screen (CRLF files stay CRLF). Switching screens with unsaved changes asks Save/Discard/Cancel; a file changed outside the editor is read again when nothing is unsaved. Penpot exports stay read-only. `--ui <file> --ui-select <node>` opens edit mode with a node selected (check "Editor UI edit (select)": nothing is written, no `UI edit:` warnings). `ScreenInfo` gives the screen's origin and scale for box → pixel mapping. `inputString` moved out of `FieldEditor.cpp`'s private part for reuse; `mealkit_editor` links `mkui` (the DLL doesn't export it). Docs: manual "Game UI > Editing screens in the editor" (English, Korean).
- **Step 3, dragging on the preview** (2026-11-04, `editor/UiPanelDrag.cpp`): the editor passes the mouse to `UiPanel::pointer` every frame while the panel edits (press, hold, Ctrl, whether an editor window has the mouse). Pressing inside the selected node keeps it selected so its children don't take the drag; a press becomes a drag after 3 pixels. Free nodes move by rewriting `pos` (rounded to whole units) with snapping of edges and centers to the parent's and siblings' boxes within 6 screen pixels (`Ctrl` turns it off) and resize from eight handles (`size`: only the dragged axis changes, a `hug`/`fill`/`$token` axis starts from the drawn size; left/top handles move `pos` too). A node whose parent has `layout` (and isn't `absolute`) reorders instead: the insert position counts siblings before the mouse by drawn center (row: x, column: y, grid: rows then x), shown as a line, applied with `moveNode` on release. Overlays go in ImGui's background list (over the scene, under editor windows): outline, handles, padding band (number values only), snap guides, insert line; the panel's old `UiQuad` outline is replaced. History commits once when the drag ends. `pos` lines with `$tokens` aren't dragged. `--ui-drag dx,dy` (with `--ui-select`) drags through the same code after the first layout and logs `UI drag test: <node> pos .. size .. index ..`; check "Editor UI edit (select, drag)" moves `panel` and reorders `resume` in `pause.ui` without saving.
- **Step 4, preview inputs** (2026-11-04): resolution, UI scale, language and live view model values already existed. New: **Save values** keeps a view model's test values in `art/ui/samples/<screen>.<ViewModel>.values` (not shipped; `reflect` text file, `@format uivalues 1`), loaded when the screen opens or the game DLL arrives (sandbox has one for `pause`). **Show state** in the properties makes the selected node blend to hover/pressed/focus/disabled/checked (`System::setPreviewState`, `--ui-state N`). **Focus** buttons make one `ui.*` move and name the focused node (`System::moveFocus`, the navigation code became `stepFocus`; `ScreenInfo::focus`; unit test `ui_preview_focus`).
- **Step 5, adding and copying nodes** (2026-11-04): **+ Frame / + Text / + Image / + Component** add a node inside the selected frame or the board, or after a selected text, image or component, with a name unique in the file. **Duplicate** (`Ctrl+D`), **Copy** and **Paste** (`Ctrl+C`/`Ctrl+V`; while the panel edits) keep the subtree; the clipboard holds plain `.ui` text, so it pastes to and from a text editor. A pasted or duplicated node gets a unique name, its children keep theirs (`label` under each button). **Wrap in frame** puts a frame with the node's drawn size in its place and moves a free node's `pos` to it. `mkui`: `writeNodeText`, `parseNodeText`, `uniqueName`, `wrapNode` (covered by `ui_source`). `tools\check_strings.ps1` compares keys case-sensitively as the editor does; that exposed three missing translations (`Copy`, `Vertical Sync`, `environment`).
- **Checks**: unit test `ui_source` writes every `.ui` in the engine repo back byte for byte (samples, module, template, Penpot expected files; FuseTag and `module-cooldown` checked the same way by hand: all already in the canonical shape), maps the sandbox screens with components, and covers property and tree edits. The Editor build's `mealkit_tests` links `mkui` directly since `mealkit.dll` exports only engine objects.

## Fixes after v0.9.0 (found while building the FuseTag example game)

- **Editor play no longer stops on `Esc`**: `Esc` goes to the game (its pause menu), as `manual/multiplayer.md` already said for games with menus; `F5` alone stops play. Strings, shortcut help and docs updated.
- **Game code is rebuilt when the project opens if its sources are newer than the game DLL** (`GameModuleHost::olderThanSources`: `source/`, `sim/`, `view/`, `app/`, `modules/`, `CMakeLists.txt`). Before, code edited while the editor was closed kept running the old DLL until a manual build.
- **Sky without an HDRI is a gradient, default fog is lighter**: with no sky HDRI the background was a flat clear to the fog color, though the editor offers it as "None (sky colors)"; the sky pass now always runs and draws the fog color at the horizon up to the sky color overhead (`PSSky`). Default sky color 0.18 0.36 0.85 (was 0.30 0.38 0.50), fog 0.55 0.70 0.95 at density 0.003 (was 0.50 0.62 0.78 at 0.008, which washed out anything past ~30 m). Sample and template scenes on the old defaults moved to the new ones (the environment isn't in the content hash).
- **Settings screen scrolls**: `scroll` and `list` on the same frame left it a list (the parser kept the last widget kind), so it clipped but never scrolled. The settings screens (sandbox, coop) now hold the list in a child frame of the scroll area, the parser rejects the combination, and keyboard/gamepad focus moves scroll the focused widget into view (only the wheel scrolled before). The wheel itself never reached a scroll area on a screen whose own panel blocks the mouse (every real screen): the screen's hit test marked the mouse taken before the wheel check; now only screens above count (`ui_layout` tests it).
- **AgX "Punchy" look, on by default** (`PostFields::punchy`, inspector "Punchy look"): AgX's base look is flat and pale, which is most of why scenes looked hazy; the look from the same AgX implementation (power 1.35, saturation 1.4) runs after the tone curve. Scenes saved before get it too (missing post fields take the default); uncheck it for the old look. Golden images updated.

## v0.9.0 — v1.0 candidate: contracts reviewed, cookbook written (2026-11-03)

Finished the v0.9 items (ROADMAP "Versions", `docs/DECISIONS.md` 2026-11-03 v1.0 scope) and bumped to v0.9.
- **Contents** (entries below, v0.8.3 ~ v0.8.4): first green Linux x64 / macOS ARM64 CI with hashes matching Windows, the contract freeze review (`docs/CONTRACTS.md`, `engine/public_headers.txt` and its check, `game::SimulationGame`, `MEALKIT_GAME_EXPORT`), the cookbook (docs repo `cookbook/`, checked against the samples).
- **From here to v1.0**: only fixes. v1.0 freezes what `docs/CONTRACTS.md` lists, when the user declares it.
- **Impact**: no template or format changes since 0.8.4; existing projects only get the new engine version from `tools\upgrade_project.ps1`. Docs repo `ENGINE_VERSION` 0.9.

## v0.8.4 — GameSession derives from game::SimulationGame (2026-11-03)

### [x] Contract freeze review item 2: the GameInstance forwarding moves into the engine (done 2026-11-03)
- **`game::SimulationGame<World>`** (`mealkit/game/SimulationGame.h`): a `GameInstance` that owns the match's `game::Simulation<World>` (protected `sim()`) and implements the 11 functions every `GameSession` wrote the same way: `started`, `nextTick`, `stateHash`, `activeBodies` (`world.physics.activeBodyCount()`), `finished`, `captureDue`, `takeSimMsPerTick`, `physics`, `setTuning` (`World::setTuning`), `stateView`, `debug`. `TuningType` comes from `World::tuning`, so `GameSession` no longer declares it. A game now writes `start`, `stop`, `update`, `buildFrame` and optionally `lobby`, `menus`, `debugUi`.
- **Its own header**, not in `Simulation.h`: `Simulation.h` is part of the simulation version, so changing the runner's file would invalidate peers and replays for an addition that runs nothing.
- **`lobby()` keeps its arguments** (session, options, user folder): solo play from the command line calls it before the view services in `GameContext` exist (`GameMain.cpp`).
- **Template change**: `samples/sandbox`, `samples/coop` and `templates/empty` derive from it (`m_sim.` became `sim().`, the forwarding lines and `setTuning` went away, about 135 lines fewer). The `GameInstance` interface and `GameModule` didn't change, so `kApiVersion` stays 22 and existing projects build as they are; `upgrade_project.ps1 -Source` brings the new form. Docs repo: game code, debug UI and engine internals pages.
- **Left**: the other freeze items (export macro, `docs/CONTRACTS.md`).

### [x] Contract freeze review item 1: public header list and check (done 2026-11-03)
- **`engine/public_headers.txt`**: the 48 engine headers game code may include, grouped (types and math, simulation tools, data and assets, game boundary and entry points, view side). It is what the samples and templates include today, plus `game/GameInstance.h` and `game/Simulation.h` (reached through `SimulationGame.h`), `sim/Ragdoll.h` (its types come through `sim/Movement.h`) and `jobs/Jobs.h` (an engine tool in `docs/CODING_MODEL.md`). Types the listed headers declare are public too, also when they come from an unlisted header. The other 77 of the 125 engine headers are internal.
- **Kept small on purpose**: adding a header later breaks nothing, removing one would wait for v2.0. Not listed for now: `online/Online.h` (no game uses online services directly; `GameContext` has none), `platform/Input.h` (key codes are for engine/debug keys only), `game/Scenario.h`, `asset/Animation.h`.
- **`tools\check_public_headers.ps1`** (check.ps1 static group, also chosen by `-Changed` for sample code, templates and the list): every `#include "mealkit/..."` in `samples/<name>/{sim,view,app,modules}` and `templates/` must be on the list, and every listed header must exist. `-Path` checks other folders: `module-cooldown` and the MealKit-Modules template pass.
- **Docs**: `docs/CODING_MODEL.md` "How it's enforced" 8; docs repo page for module authors.

### [x] Contract freeze review item 3: game library export macro (done 2026-11-03)
- `MEALKIT_GAME_MODULE` exported `mealkitGetGameModule` with `__declspec(dllexport)`, which Clang/GCC outside Windows reject without `-fdeclspec`. `MEALKIT_GAME_EXPORT` (`game/GameInstance.h`) is `__declspec(dllexport)` on Windows and `__attribute__((visibility("default")))` elsewhere. Game code doesn't change (the macro's body isn't part of the contract). The non-Windows branch is first compiled by the Linux/macOS editor build in v1.x (CI builds only headless targets there).

### [x] Contract freeze review item 4: the contract document (done 2026-11-03)
- **`docs/CONTRACTS.md`**: the game API (public headers, source compatible, what v1.x may add), every frozen file with its `@format` version at the freeze (project files and player-side files: saves, `settings.ini`, the player's `input.bindings`), the simulation rules (SimCheck errors fixed, new rules warn), and what isn't covered (simulation results across versions, replays of another simulation version, cooked and baked files).
- **Docs repo**: reference page "What stays the same in v1.x" (`reference/contracts.md`, English and Korean) for game authors, linking to the engine document.
- With items 1~4 the contract freeze review is done; the cookbook docs are the last v0.9 item.

### [x] Cookbook docs (done 2026-11-03, docs repo)
- **Four recipes** in the docs repo's `cookbook/` (English and Korean), each built from reference sample code: cooldowns and timers (coop shove, `TickTimer` + `Ticks` tuning), adding a new input (sandbox push: `input.bindings` row, tick input bit, `LocalInput` latch, `sim/` read), rounds and countdown (sandbox `MatchFlow`: rules from tuning, `advanceMatch` events, countdown tick events), sound and effects on a game event (tick event + `feedback.table` row).
- **They break visibly when a sample changes**: a `% from: <engine path>` line before each code block names the sample file, and the docs `build.py check --engine` fails when a line of the block is no longer in that file (15 blocks today). The docs CI's engine checkout now includes the samples' `sim/`, `view/`, `app/` and tables.
- With the contract freeze review this finishes the v0.9 items (the version bump is the user's call).

## v0.8.3 — publication and the first green Linux/macOS CI (2026-11-03)

### [x] First green public CI on Linux x64 and macOS ARM64 (2026-11-03, commit 9dc8a42)
- **Result**: unit tests pass on Linux x64 (Clang) and macOS ARM64 (Apple Clang); `detmath-bits` and the per-tick hashes of the sandbox and coop replays match Windows MSVC and clang-cl bit for bit; coop scenarios and headless `mp_test` pass. This is the first evidence that the simulation gives the same results on ARM.
- **Headless-only build fixes** (Linux/macOS CI build only `mealkit_core`, no front-end target): `MEALKIT_LIVE` defines only when the `mealkit` target exists; tests that need the front end (bug/crash reports, audio reverb zones) behind `MEALKIT_TEST_FRONT`; float `std::from_chars` (missing in Apple libc++) replaced by `std::strtof` in `AnimStates.cpp`/`Cutscene.cpp`; headless exes get the build-time project folder so they find their sample assets.
- **LAN rooms on Linux** (a real bug, not only CI): every room finder on a PC shares the room port with `SO_REUSEPORT`, and Linux delivers a unicast datagram to only one of those sockets, so a second game or the Manager on the same PC could miss rooms. The announcer now also sends to `127.255.255.255` (`net::kLoopbackBroadcastIp`), which reaches every socket; the duplicate is merged by the existing room de-duplication. Unit test `lobby_chat_rematch` covers it.
- **CI** builds `sandbox_headless`, `coop_headless` and `mealkit_tests` on Linux/macOS (coop was missing, so its hashes were empty).
- **Same build on Windows**: `build.bat HeadlessOnly` (clang-cl, `-DMEALKIT_FORCE_HEADLESS_ONLY=ON`) reproduces the Linux/macOS configuration, then `tools\ci_test.ps1 -Bin build\HeadlessOnly\bin -Name local -Out build\ci-local` (in `CONTRIBUTING.md`).
- **Left**: the same between real devices (ROADMAP stage 5, now v1.x per `docs/DECISIONS.md` 2026-11-03).

## v0.8.3 — Game QA 2: scenario tests, mp_test and reports as scenarios in MealKit QA (2026-11-02)

### [x] Tool suite: Game QA 2 (done 2026-11-02, MealKit-QA 0.2.0)
- **Engine**: a scenario without `tick` lines is accepted only with `--update-expected`, which appends one `==` check per top-level scalar state value (not view-only) at the last executed tick (`game::scenarioChecksText`, shared with editor [Save Scenario], which now calls it). `loadScenario(..., allowNoChecks)`; output `SCENARIO updated` + `SCENARIO ok <file> checks=N`. Unit test `game/scenario` covers it.
- **MealKit QA** (separate repo, still no engine link): a **Tests** page lists `<project>/tests/*.scenario` and runs them with `<name>_headless --test` (Run All, one, Update Expected; failed check lines, file, output), and runs `tools/mp_test.ps1 -Exe <name>.exe -Headless -Clients N -Ticks T`. Processes run one at a time on a worker thread (`tool/Runner`, SDL_Process). Executables come from the build root's `build/Release/bin` (the engine repo for `samples/<name>`, the same rule as `ProjectInfo::buildRoot`).
- **Save as Scenario** on a report or replay: copies the recording to `tests/<kind>_<name>.rec`, writes `tests/<kind>_<name>.scenario` (`inputs`, `ticks` = report tick + 1, `scene` from `info.txt`) and runs it with `--update-expected` to fill the checks. Without a window: `MealKitQA --run-tests [--mp-test]`, `--make-scenario <report name>`.
- **Checked**: sandbox `--run-tests --mp-test` → 3 scenarios ok, `PASS: 4 peers identical after 900 ticks`; a saved replay became a passing scenario (then removed).
- **Left**: the sandbox's top-level state has only `tick` and `playerCount` as scalars, so a scenario saved from a report starts with two checks; the useful ones (per-player values in arrays/pools) are written by hand. Proposing nested values (e.g. per slot) is a candidate if that proves tedious. Old bug reports from before recordings were bundled can't be saved (no `replay.rec`).

## v0.8.2 — UI 1: game UI runtime split inside the engine (2026-11-02)

### [x] Tool suite: UI 1, splitting the UI runtime layer inside the engine (done 2026-11-02)
- **Runtime** `engine/libs/mkui/` (temporary name like `mkaudio` was, own `CMakeLists.txt`, `include/mkui/`, `src/`, namespace `mkui`): `.ui`/theme reading (`Document`), font SDF baking and atlas packing (`Atlas`, stb_truetype compiled here), text shaping (`Text`) and the retained screens with layout, bindings, input and drawing (`mkui::System`) moved over from `mealkit/ui/`. Its own `Types.h` (`u32`, `f32`, `rgba()`), "@format ui N" lines through mkcontract. Output is `std::vector<mkui::Quad>`, the same 112-byte layout as `render::UiQuad` (the glue checks sizes/offsets/flags with `static_assert` and hands the array to `Renderer::drawUi` as is).
- **Interfaces the glue fills** (`mkui/Host.h`): `Host` (read/stamp/list asset files, logs, string table text, image decoding, atlas uploads) and `Binder` (resolve a field path in an opaque `Model` into a `Value`: kind, size, data, enum names, ticks per second, array element type/size; type names; formatting other kinds). `FrameInput` is plain mouse/typing/navigation values instead of `Input`/`input::Actions`; `setCamera` takes 16 floats, `drawAt` x/y/z.
- **Glue** `mealkit/ui/Ui.cpp` (1711 → about 200 lines): `EngineHost` (`findAssetFile`/`fileWriteTime`/`listFiles`, `MEALKIT_INFO/WARN`, `loc::Strings`, stb_image, `Renderer::updateUiAtlas`), `ReflectBinder` (`reflect::resolvePath`, `formatScalar`, `tickRate`), input mapping. `ui::System`'s API is unchanged (`Action`/`ScreenInfo`/`NodeBox`/`ScreenId` are aliases of the mkui types); `mealkit/ui/Document.h` keeps `ui::loadDocument`/`parseDocument`/`loadTheme` for the editor [UI] panel, `--ui-check` and tests. `ui/StbImpl.cpp` keeps only stb_image.
- **Allocation-free UI frames** (the follow-up from v0.7.20): benchmark `allocs` 112.9 → 4.9 per frame (UI part about 0.2, from the 0.25 s file change checks; the rest is game code in `buildFrame`, about 3). Bound text is resolved into a kept buffer with `string_view` parsing and numbers formatted into stack buffers (`appendValue`), paths built from pieces (`<field>Count`, `!field`) go through one kept string, text shaping reuses its run/glyph/line/code point vectors, layout temporaries (flow lists, flex items/lines, grid cells/tracks) live in per-depth `Scratch` buffers, drawing blends a plain `Look` instead of copying `Visual` (shadow vectors, token strings), and world UI (name tags) rebinds the template's list items to each view model instead of rebuilding the node array every draw (that was most of the remaining 25). `engine/tests/perf/baseline.txt` `allocs` 113 → 5.
- **Checks**: `tools\check_libs.ps1` also scans `engine/libs/*`; `check.ps1 -Changed` maps `engine/libs/mkui/` to the `ui` folder groups. `check.ps1 -Changed` passed 47/48 (Penpot screens = engine layout/pixels, game screen flow, graphics regression on DX12 and Vulkan); the one warning was the noisy p99 timing (4.1-5.8 ms across repeated runs with the same build).
- **Left**: UI 2 (own repo, CI, deviceless tests; earliest after the `.ui` format freeze); the reload checks' `findAssetFile` strings every 0.25 s; game code allocations in the samples' `buildFrame`.

## v0.8.0 — stage 4 done (2026-11-02)

Finished the last v0.8-blocking item of stage 4 (the second reference sample) and bumped to v0.8 (ROADMAP "Versions": v0.n = the engine that finished stage n).
- **Stage 4 contents** (entries below, v0.7.1 ~ v0.7.22): source modules, scenario tests, namespace cleanup, game flow, movement modes, active ragdoll, animation state table, virtual cameras + cutscenes, AI pathfinding, prefabs, heightmap terrain, Job System, spatial audio + interactive music, asset cooking + pack files, performance baseline, optimization candidates 1~4 and 9, empty project template, `samples/coop`.

## v0.7.22 — Second reference sample: co-op third person (2026-11-02)

### [x] Stage 4: Second reference sample, co-op third person (done 2026-11-02)
- **Samples are projects** (`docs/DECISIONS.md` 2026-11-02): the root `mealkit.project`, `assets/`, `art/` and `modules/` moved to `samples/sandbox/`. `mealkit_add_game` uses the calling folder as the project folder, adds one `<name>_export` per game under `game_export`, and cooks next to the build folder (`build/cook/<name>.pak`). Dev builds write `MEALKIT_BUILD_PROJECT_DIR` into `mealkit_build_info.h` (empty with `MEALKIT_LIVE` off, so shipping exes don't carry the path) and `game::useBuildProjectDirectory` sets the asset root from it. The editor builds a sample's game DLL with the engine's `build.bat` (`ProjectInfo::buildRoot`). `editor.bat sample` opens `samples\sandbox`. Tools follow: `check.ps1` (`$sandbox`, path rules `samples/<name>/assets/...`, the determinism key hashes every sample's content), `check_layout.ps1`, `live_probe.ps1`, `mp_test.ps1` (desync dumps in `samples\<exe>\build\desync`), `new_project.ps1`/`upgrade_project.ps1`/`project_common.ps1` (arena assets and modules from `samples\sandbox`), the sample asset generators, and `mealkit_tests` (asset root `samples/sandbox`).
- **`samples/coop/`**: 1-4 heroes cross the hill terrain (`scenes/hills.scene`, the sandbox heightmap and scatter layers) to a goal while chasers hunt them. Heroes are active ragdolls (all three `bodies.table` rows); a chaser that touches a hero knocks them limp (`knockRagdoll`, kept limp while `downed`) and rests; a teammate standing within `reviveRadius` for `reviveTime` lifts them up; shoving (push button) stuns chasers nearby. Chasers (kinematic, skinned figure with the animation state table) pick the nearest hero in sight every `aiThink` and follow nav grid paths baked from the terrain and tree hulls, or wander. Everyone down at once or time up loses; every connected hero up inside `GoalArea` for `goalHold` wins. Scene objects `MatchArea`, `GoalArea`, `ChaserSpawn`. View: third-person camera brain with the intro cutscene on the countdown and the `results` virtual camera, a goal beam, grey downed heroes with a revive bar, HUD lines for being down (revive %), distance to the goal and the outcome; results rank by revives. `scenes/practice.scene` has no chasers. Not a template.
- **Engine fixes found by the sample**: `MatchRules::coop` + `sim::finishRound()` (above; unit test `sim/match_flow`). Scenario `scene` lines were looked up as project paths (`findAsset`) instead of asset paths, so they silently ran the start scene; now `findAssetFile`, and a missing scene fails the test.
- **Tests**: `samples/coop/tests/` (match flow, the solo bot caught and losing, the bot winning on the practice scene, 3 players from `replays/hills_3p_seed7.rec` with a revive). `check.ps1`: "Determinism coop (mp_test 1+3 headless, 1800 ticks)", "Game capture coop (shadow + rewind)", "Editor play coop (2 players)", and "Export build" now checks every sample's export runs a bot match and has no dev path. The bot input heads for the goal corner and pauses, so recordings reach catches and revives.
- **Awkward, left for later** (the sample shows them, no engine change yet): `GameSession` is about 330 lines of which most (hitstop freeze, menu camera, name tags, network HUD, save timer) is the same in both samples and the empty template; bot input only sees the tick, so bots can't react to the world; the shared results screen is versus-shaped (a co-op outcome lives in the HUD); ragdoll heroes are drawn as boxes because skinned models can't follow ragdoll parts yet.

## v0.7.20 — Frame allocation check (2026-11-02)

### [x] Stage 4: Optimization candidate 9, frame allocation check (done 2026-11-02)
- **Counter** (`debug/AllocCount.h`): the dev game exe replaces the global `operator new`/`delete` (all forms, same `malloc`/`_aligned_malloc` heap as the CRT) with one that counts per thread (`alloc::threadCount()`). Only compiled with `MEALKIT_ALLOC_COUNT` for the static dev exe (`MEALKIT_DEBUG_UI` and not the Editor build), so `mealkit.dll` and Export builds are unchanged; there `alloc::counting()` is false.
- **Benchmark**: the game loop counts the main thread's allocations from `buildFrame` to `renderer.endFrame()` (render + UI). The report gets an `allocations` line (avg per frame, max, frames with any) and the `BENCHMARK` line ` allocs=<avg>/<max>`.
- **Check** (`check.ps1` "Performance baseline"): `allocs` in `engine/tests/perf/baseline.txt`; a rise over the baseline (+1) warns. Allocation counts don't depend on the PC, so this is compared even when the adapter differs (the timing values still SKIP). `-UpdatePerf` writes it too.
- **Found**: 112.9 allocations per frame (max 197), all in game UI drawing (`ui::System::render`): `resolveText` builds the bound text with new strings each frame (`substr`, `formatValue`, `"no field"` paths), plus the screen `order` vector. `DrawList`/`Renderer` allocate nothing. The baseline is 113 for now, so the check catches new allocations; making the UI path allocation-free is a follow-up (goal 0, as ROADMAP says).

## v0.7.19 — Vertex quantization (2026-11-02)

### [x] Stage 4: Optimization candidate 2, vertex quantization (done 2026-11-02)
- **`GpuVertex`** (`Renderer.cpp`, 24 bytes; was 36): position `Snorm16x4` relative to the mesh's box, normal octahedral `Snorm16x2`, color, UV (half2), material, padding. Skinned vertices 44 → 32 bytes. `createMesh` computes the box (a flat axis gets extent 1) and packs on upload; the CPU `Vertex`, hull points and cooked models are unchanged.
- **RHI**: `VertexFormat::Snorm16x4`/`Snorm16x2` (DX12 `R16G16B16A16_SNORM`/`R16G16_SNORM`, Vulkan the same).
- **Shader** (`Forward.hlsl`): `ForwardPush` gains `boxCenter`/`boxExtent` (64 bytes), set per draw by `GpuMesh::setBox` in the forward, shadow and skinned paths; `decodeOctahedral` for the normal.
- **Measured** (`--benchmark`, Intel(R) Graphics): GPU 2.12 → 2.15/2.19 ms, GPU memory 75 MB unchanged (both noise level); golden images (DX12/Vulkan) pass. Kept for bandwidth-bound min-spec integrated graphics, not yet measured there (`docs/DECISIONS.md` 2026-11-02).

## v0.7.18 — Pipeline cache on disk (2026-11-02)

### [x] Stage 4: Optimization candidate 4, pipeline cache on disk (done 2026-11-02)
- **`rhi::DeviceDesc::pipelineCache`** (folder, empty = off; `RendererDesc` passes it): DX12 keeps an `ID3D12PipelineLibrary1` in `pipelines.dx12`, Vulkan a `VkPipelineCache` in `pipelines.vulkan`, read on init and written on shutdown (temp file + rename). The game uses `%LOCALAPPDATA%\<game>\cache`, the editor `%LOCALAPPDATA%\MealKit Editor\cache`.
- **Keys** (`rhi/PipelineCache.h`, `pipelineKey`): hash of both shaders' bytecode and every `PipelineDesc` field. The DX12 file starts with the list of stored keys, so only stored pipelines are looked up (a library miss is a debug layer warning).
- **Other drivers/GPUs**: D3D12 refuses a library from another driver on creation (then an empty one is used); Vulkan's cache header (vendor, device, `pipelineCacheUUID`) is checked before use. Pipelines rebuilt by shader hot reload bypass the cache, so the file doesn't grow during development.
- **Editor**: `GameModuleHost::olderThanEngine` ignores headers under `rhi/dx12`, `rhi/vulkan` (game code never includes them); before, editing a backend header made the other backend's editor rebuild game code on start.
- **Measured** (Intel(R) Graphics): startup with `--frames 2` was about 700 ms with or without the file, because the driver's own shader cache was warm; the first run with a cold driver cache took 1026 ms. The gain is on first start after install or driver updates and on first use of a pipeline.

## v0.7.17 — Small-object distance cull (2026-11-02)

### [x] Stage 4: Optimization candidate 3, small-object distance cull (done 2026-11-02)
- **`ModelCache::pickLod`**: the same screen size measure as LOD choice (bounding radius / distance) is compared with the model kind's `ModelBudget::cullSize`; below it, `pickLod` returns `render::kNoMesh`, which `DrawList::add`/`addSkinned` and `asset::addModel` skip. So scene objects, scatter layers and the editor view all use it.
- **Project settings** `cullCharactersSize`, `cullPropsSize`, `cullEnvironmentSize` (passed with the model budgets, `game::modelBudgets`). Defaults: props 0.004 (about 2.5 px radius at 720p), characters and environment 0 (off).
- **Measured** (`--benchmark`, Intel(R) Graphics): GPU 2.10 ms vs 2.12~2.19 before; the sample has few small props, so the gain shows in scatter-heavy scenes. Golden images unchanged.
- **Not yet**: no dissolve fade at the cull distance (dissolve only works on dissolve materials), so objects pop; distance-culled instances aren't counted in the `culled` stat.

## v0.7.16 — Front-to-back draw order (2026-11-02)

### [x] Stage 4: Optimization candidate 1, front-to-back bucket order (done 2026-11-02)
- **`DrawList::submit`**: after culling, opaque buckets are drawn by their nearest visible instance (squared distance to `Renderer::cameraPos()`), and buckets with an alpha mask or dissolve material (`Renderer::meshAlphaClip`, set in `createMesh`) go last because discard turns off early depth. Buckets with up to 256 visible instances are also sorted inside by distance; larger ones (scatter grass) keep add order so the per-frame sort stays small. Ties fall back to mesh index, so the order is stable. Shadows and skinned meshes are unchanged.
- **Measured** (`--benchmark 10`, Intel(R) Graphics): GPU 2.17 → 2.13/2.14 ms (about 2 %), avg frame 2.33 → 2.30/2.31 ms. The sample arena has little overdraw, so the gain is small; it grows with dense scenes. Golden images (DX12/Vulkan) unchanged.

## v0.7.15 — Performance baseline check (2026-11-02)

### [x] Stage 4: Performance baseline check (done 2026-11-02)
- **Check item** (`tools/check.ps1`, `game` group, "Performance baseline"): runs `sandbox.exe --benchmark 10 --mute` (fixed seed, bots, vsync off, 3 s warmup), reads the `BENCHMARK` line and the report's GPU adapter, and compares avg/p99 frame, sim ms per tick, GPU frame ms and peak process/GPU memory with `engine/tests/perf/baseline.txt` (`key value` lines: `adapter`, `tolerance` in %, then the values). Flagged when over `baseline * (1 + tolerance)` and over an absolute slack (0.5 ms, 32 MB), so tiny values don't warn on noise. `worst` frame isn't compared (one hitch decides it).
- **Warning only**: a new `WARN: reason` result (yellow, counted as passed; the summary prints `warned`). A baseline from another adapter gives `SKIP`, so CI and other PCs don't compare. Each run's report is `build\check\benchmark.txt`, the per-value comparison is in the step log.
- **`-UpdatePerf`**: adds the `game` group and writes this run as the baseline (`WARN: baseline updated`). Path rule `engine/tests/perf/` → `game`.
- **`BENCHMARK` line**: adds `mem=<MB> gpumem=<MB>` (peak process/GPU memory).
- **Baseline** (this PC, Intel(R) Graphics, 1280×720 default window): avg 2.33 ms, p99 4.12 ms, sim 1.2 ms, GPU 2.17 ms, 255 MB, GPU 75 MB. Two repeat runs stayed within 3 % (avg 2.27/2.29, GPU 2.18/2.17); one run during other PC load reached avg 3.07 ms / p99 10.5 ms, which is why it only warns.
- **Docs**: docs repo "Working on the engine > Testing" > "Performance baseline" (English/Korean).
- **Not yet**: the baseline is not the minimum-spec 11th gen Xe measurement unless run there; no Vulkan baseline; no per-pass GPU comparison (the report has it).

## v0.7.14 — Asset cooking + pack files (2026-11-02)

### [x] Stage 4: Asset cooking + pack files (done 2026-11-02)
- **Pack files** (`platform/Pack.h`, core): one `<name>.pak` (header, entry data, path table; entries LZ4 or stored, decided per file). While a pack is mounted, the existing file functions serve paths under the pack's folder from it: `findAsset` looks only in the pack (no parent-folder search, so a missing file fails the same in every build), `readTextFile`, `openFile` (read modes: a `tmpfile` copy for `FILE*` readers like scenes and scatter files), `listFiles`/`listDirectories`, `fileWriteTime`. Asset code didn't change. Several packs can be mounted (later wins), for patches later.
- **Cooked assets** (`asset/Cooked.h`): glTF → `<path>.mkmodel` (optimized LOD meshes, hull points and bounds from the source vertices, skeleton, baked clips, materials, BC7/BC5 textures inside), cutscene cameras → `<path>.mkcam`, sky HDRIs → `<path>.mkenv` (the baked cache file). Readers try the source first, then the cooked file, so the editor keeps the sources. Magic + version + struct-size check; a mismatch says "cook again".
- **Import split**: glTF reading moved from `ModelCache.cpp` to `asset/ModelImport.cpp` (`importModel` → `CookedModel`, the same path dev builds load through), compiled only with `MEALKIT_ASSET_IMPORT` (debug UI builds, editor, headless). The shipping exe has no cgltf or meshoptimizer: 3.7 MB → 3.6 MB (3622 KB).
- **Cook step** (`game/Cook.h`, `<game>.exe --cook <out.pak>` from a dev build): starts from the start scene, project setting `scenes` and `packInclude` (files or folders), plus every data file opened by fixed name or folder (tables, tuning, bindings, sound events, effects, UI, strings, fonts); then any word in a reached text asset that is an existing asset path is reached too (scene → models/prefabs/terrain/HDRI/probes, `sounds.events` → clips, `.ui` → images, `cutscenes.table` → camera glTF). String literals in game source naming an unreached asset are warned about. `mealkit.project` goes in without the dev PC lines. `<out.pak>.txt` has the content hash and the list. The sample packs 52 of 73 assets (2.3 MB); `rig_figure.glb` is set only as a `ViewTuning` default, so the sample's `mealkit.project` lists it in `packInclude`.
- **Export**: `build.bat Export` builds Release first, cooks with it, and ships exe + `<name>.pak` + `build.txt` + notices (+ Steam/EOS DLL). No assets folder, no `cache/` folders. `build.txt` = engine version, project git commit (`+` if modified), pack content hash, build time; `--help` prints it and bug reports include it.
- **Shipping exe**: mounts every `*.pak` next to it at startup; `--pack <file>` mounts one in a dev build (play the cooked game without exporting). Sound: mkaudio 0.3.1 `Desc::readFile` (MealKit-Audio), set when a pack is mounted, so clips and streamed music come from the pack.
- **Checked**: unit tests `platform/pack_files`, `asset/cooked_assets` (import ↔ cooked bytes, cooked model loaded through a pack); `sandbox --seed 7 --bot --exit-tick 300` gives the same hash from sources, from `--pack`, and from the exported exe; `check.ps1 -Changed` (43 groups); mkaudio tests (89).
- **Not yet**: editor play on the cooked version (only the game exe's `--pack`); headless exes don't mount packs; scenes and other text stay text inside the pack (only compressed); per-platform texture formats (ASTC) come with stage 5; async loading stays deferred (`docs/DECISIONS.md` 2026-10-29).

## v0.7.13 — Spatial audio and interactive music (2026-11-02)

### [x] Stage 4: Spatial audio and interactive music (done 2026-11-02)
- **Runtime** (MealKit-Audio v0.3.0, pulled with `tools\update_mkaudio.ps1 -Ref v0.3.0`): per-voice occlusion (`emitters()` → `setOcclusion(handle, 0..1)`, eased over 0.12 s; the event's `occlusionVolume`/`occlusionLowpass` when fully blocked). Each bus gets one FX node after it: snapshot low-pass (2-pole), reverb (own Freeverb, public-domain algorithm, only on buses with `reverb true`; `setReverb(values, seconds)` moves to new values), peak meter. Ducking (`duckBy`, `duckVolume`, attack/release) from voices or music sounding on the trigger bus or its children. Snapshots (`snapshot <name>`: bus volume/low-pass, `fade`; several multiply). Music tracks (`music <name>`: `stems`, `bpm`, `beatsPerBar`, `offset`, `transition` immediate/beat/bar, `crossfade`, `stemParam` with range and `stemFade`): `playMusic` schedules the new track's start and the old track's stop+fade to the PCM frame of the next beat/bar (`ma_sound_set_start_time`/`stop_time_with_fade`, 50 ms lookahead); stem 0 always plays, later stems come in one by one as the global parameter rises; `stinger(event, Beat|Bar)` starts a 2D event on the music's grid. Event file `@format audio-events 2` (version 1 reads as is). 83 deviceless checks (occlusion loudness, reverb tail, ducking, snapshot volume/low-pass, bar-exact switch, stinger on the beat, stems by parameter, format round trip).
- **Authoring tool**: Music window (tracks, stems from `assets/`, tempo, transition, stem parameter; preview play/stop with bar/beat, parameter slider, stingers on beat/bar), Mix window (snapshots with preview on/off, reverb preview), bus effects popup (reverb, ducked by), occlusion values per event and an occlusion preview slider. New windows dock beside Event even with an old saved layout.
- **Engine glue** (`audio/Audio.h`): `playMusic`/`stopMusic`/`stinger`/`music()`, `setSnapshot`, `setOcclusionWorld` + `kOcclusionRaysPerFrame` (4 mixed 3D voices per frame in turn, a Jolt ray from the listener stopping 1.5 m short of the sound so its own body doesn't hide it; read on the main thread after the game's update, between ticks), `setReverbZones` (smallest zone around the listener, rotated boxes), `resetMatch`, `spatialStats()`. The live `audio/state` adds occlusion per voice, duck/snapshot per bus, music, snapshots, spatial.
- **Scene**: built-in kind `reverb_zone` (area + cosmetic: outside the content hash; `ReverbZoneFields` room size, damping, wet, width, fade).
- **Game boundary**: `GameInstance::physics()` (game API 22: game DLLs need a rebuild). The game exe and editor play pass it to audio every frame (so it never points at a finished match) and the scene's zones at match start; editor play stop calls `resetMatch`.
- **Sample**: music is the track `arena` (three stems from `make_sample_sounds.py`: bass, drums, arpeggio) with `tension` following the match phase, a `stinger` on the next bar at round end (voice bus, ducks the music), snapshot `pause` while menus cover the match, reverb on `sfx`, reverb zone "Echo Corner" in `arena.scene`.
- **Checked**: mkaudio tests (83), unit tests `audio/audio_reverb_zones` and `scene/scene_reverb_zone`, the sample over the live connection (music on the bar grid, stems by tension, occlusion rays and occluded voices behind cover), the tool's new windows.
- **Not yet**: occlusion is one ray, blocked or not (no partial/diffraction); reverb is a per-bus insert, not per-voice sends; music transitions don't have authored exit cues or transition segments; the zone isn't drawn differently from other areas in the editor.

## v0.7.12 — Job System (2026-11-02)

### [x] Stage 4: Job System (done 2026-11-02)
- **Engine** `jobs/Jobs.h` (new folder `jobs`, right after `core`/`platform` in the layer order; core sources, so headless/Linux/macOS have it): one shared worker pool = Jolt's `JobSystemThreadPool` (no new library; decided with the user, `docs/DECISIONS.md` 2026-11-02). Reference-counted `acquire`/`release` (core count - 1 workers, at most 6; explicit so no thread is joined during DLL unload), `parallelFor(count, minChunk, fn)` (the calling thread takes the first chunk and helps through the barrier), `run(fn)` → `Task` polled with `isReady()`, `joltJobSystem()` for physics.
- **Physics**: `PhysicsWorld::init(maxBodies, 0)` (the default) steps on the shared pool, so shadow/rewind worlds no longer each start their own threads; an explicit thread count still makes a private pool (thread count tests). Results don't depend on the pool (Jolt `CROSS_PLATFORM_DETERMINISTIC`).
- **Render**: `render::cullInstances` (DrawList.h) culls all buckets in parallel per instance (balanced when one bucket, e.g. scatter grass, holds most instances) and compacts in bucket order, so draws are identical to the serial loop. Used by `DrawList::submit` (main view) and `Renderer::drawShadows` (each cascade). The renderer holds the pool from `init` to `shutdown`.
- **Checked**: unit test `jobs/jobs_parallel_for` (each index once, inline without a pool, Task); `check.ps1 -Changed` (43 groups incl. determinism, shadow/rewind, golden DX12/Vulkan).
- **Not yet**: animation poses are still sampled one by one in `asset::addModel` as the game calls it (parallel poses need a batched add API; goes with "Animation LOD" in the optimization candidates); no `--benchmark` before/after numbers yet (the performance baseline item).

## v0.7.11 — Scatter brush (2026-11-02)

### [x] Stage 4: Heightmap terrain, part 2: scatter brush (done 2026-11-02)
- **Scene kind** `scatter` (scatter layer, `kObjectCosmetic`: out of the content hash, collision and simulation). Fields `ScatterFields`: model, instance file, density (per m²), smallest/largest size, random rotation, draw distance. The layer object's own transform isn't used.
- **Engine** `asset/Scatter.h`: instances (world position, yaw, uniform scale) in `assets/terrain/<scene>_<id>.scatter` (`@format scatter 1`, one `i x y z yaw scale` line each). `paintScatter` (random points in the circle that find floor, min spacing 0.75/√density), `eraseScatter`, `groupScatter` (32 m cells). `ModelCache::scatter`/`saveScatters`/`addScatter`: cells beyond the draw distance are skipped whole, the rest go per instance through the draw list (frustum culling, model LOD). Without a model a small box stands in.
- **Editor**: [+ Type...] > Scatter layer (brush on), inspector [Scatter]: instance count, Brush, Paint/Erase (Shift inverts), radius, strength, Clear. Painting rays down onto everything in the scene (terrain surfaces and solid boxes/models), one stroke = one undo step (snapshots carry the instances), scene save writes the files. Layers have no gizmo and aren't picked in the viewport (scene tree).
- **Sample**: `terrain.scene` gets grass (3000) and rock (120) layers written by `tools/make_sample_terrain.py`; golden shot `terrain_hills` updated.
- **Checked**: unit test `asset/scatter_layer` (spacing, circle, floor only, grouping, file round trip, erase); bot play capture on the terrain scene; `check.ps1 -Changed`.
- **Not yet**: painting by hand isn't tested automatically; per-instance tilt to the slope; GPU instancing beyond the current per-mesh batches (the draw list already draws one call per mesh).

## v0.7.10 — Heightmap terrain (2026-11-02)

### [x] Stage 4: Heightmap terrain, part 1: heights, collision, chunk LOD, brushes (done 2026-11-02)
- **Scene kind** `terrain` (`scene::kTypeTerrain`, field `heightmap` = 16-bit PNG relative to assets, new kind folder `assets/terrain/`). The heights fill the object's box: black = box bottom, white = top, the image's top row is the +z edge. Rotation is ignored (axis-aligned). Any square size from 2 samples.
- **Engine** `asset/Terrain.h` (core, so headless servers load it too): PNG decode/encode with core-side static copies of stb_image and stb_image_write (only its deflate is used to write 16-bit grayscale), `terrainHeights`, `terrainHeightAt`/`raycastTerrain` (same triangle diagonal as Jolt), `buildTerrainChunk` (32-cell chunks, 3 LODs by distance, skirts against LOD cracks), `applyBrush` (raise/lower/flatten/smooth with smooth falloff).
- **Collision**: `PhysicsWorld::createHeightField` (Jolt `HeightFieldShape`, padded to an even sample count with no-collision samples). The sandbox `loadScene` creates it for terrain objects; NavGrid bakes on it unchanged. **Content hash**: `ModelCache::hashModels` adds each terrain's sample hash (in-memory samples, so editor edits count too).
- **View**: `ModelCache::terrain`/`addTerrain` build chunk meshes on first draw (existing Forward shader, no RHI or shader changes); the draw list culls per chunk. Brush edits rebuild only touched chunks via the new `Renderer::replaceMesh` + `rhi::Device::destroyBuffer` (DX12 and Vulkan; waits for the GPU, outside a frame, editor only in practice).
- **Editor**: [+ Type...] > Terrain makes a flat 129-sample terrain (128 x 32 x 128 m, a quarter up) saved to `terrain/<scene>_<id>.png` on scene save. Inspector [Terrain]: info, Import PNG..., Brush toggle (Raise/Lower/Flatten/Smooth, radius, strength). Left drag paints with an outline on the surface, the gizmo hides while painting, a stroke is one undo step (snapshots carry terrain samples). Picking hits the surface, not the box. Externally re-exported PNGs reload (unless edited).
- **Sample**: `assets/scenes/terrain.scene` + `assets/terrain/hills.png` (`tools/make_sample_terrain.py`), golden shot `terrain_hills`.
- **Checked**: unit test `asset/terrain_heightmap` (PNG round trip, surface height = physics ray = terrain ray, chunk vertex counts per LOD, brush radius); bot play on the terrain scene (Pawns stand on the hills); editor capture; `check.ps1 -Changed`.
- **Not yet**: scatter brush (v0.7.11), terrain materials/texture painting (one color + lighting), brush strokes are not tested automatically (only by hand).
- **Template**: sample `sim/World.cpp`/`view/Draw.cpp` code is the new-project code (VERSION 0.7.10).

## v0.7.9 — active ragdoll movement mode (2026-11-02)

### [x] Stage 4: Active ragdoll movement mode (done 2026-11-02)
- **Engine physics** (`physics/PhysicsWorld.h`): motorized joints `createJoint`/`destroyJoint`/`setJointTarget`/`setJointTorque` (Jolt `SwingTwistConstraint`, cone + twist limits, position motors toward "child = parent × target"). Joints are stored with body-space frames so they can be recreated in any pose; the engine's joint list mirrors Jolt's constraint array (same swap-remove), and `restore` rebuilds every joint in snapshot order before `RestoreState` (Jolt restores constraint state by index). Joints are in `hashState`, `writeState` (`joint <id> <parent> <child> target .. torque ..`), `save`/`restore` and snapshot bytes. `BodyDesc.group`: bodies of the same nonzero group never collide (`GroupFilter`), `castRay` can ignore a group.
- **Engine ragdoll** (`sim/Ragdoll.h`, header only): one fixed humanoid of 11 parts (pelvis/chest boxes, head sphere, capsule limbs) sized from the character height, 10 joints, created in a fixed order. `RagdollPose` (a target rotation per joint), `ragdollGait` (procedural walk from a phase), `driveRagdoll`, `senseRagdoll` (part transforms into state for the view), `ragdollPartSize` (view helper).
- **Movement mode** `MoveMode::Ragdoll` (`sim/Movement.h`): the pelvis steers like Floating (spring toward `ragdollHover` over a ray that ignores its own parts), the rest of the body is weightless while held up, the pelvis is turned upright and toward the move direction by angular velocity from the quaternion error (arithmetic only), the gait phase advances by distance walked. `knockRagdoll` makes it limp (`ragdollLimp` torque, no balance or hover) for N ticks plus a velocity change; afterwards the spring and upright control stand it back up. New `MoveTuning` fields `ragdoll*`, `Mover.ragdoll`, `MoverShape.group`.
- **Sandbox**: `bodies.table` row `ragdoll` (movement Ragdoll), pushing knocks nearby ragdoll Pawns down for tuning `knockdown` (1.2 s; other modes still aren't pushed), `spawnPawn` creates the Pawn first so its slot gives the collision group, `drawWorld` draws ragdoll parts as boxes.
- **Cost**: 4 ragdolls (44 bodies, 40 joints) take about 0.12 ms per tick (move + step + sense, Release, 2 physics threads), far within the 4 ms tick budget, so the full ragdoll is kept (no reduced version).
- **Checked**: unit test `sim/ragdoll_movement` (4 ragdolls stand upright at the right height, walk, one is knocked down and stands up again; a snapshot taken before an extra ragdoll comes and goes with out-of-order joint removal restores in the same world, and as bytes into a 4-thread world, with the same per-tick hashes); `check.ps1 -Changed`; a bot capture with the first body row temporarily set to Ragdoll. Replay `arena_4p_seed7.rec` re-recorded (Pawn state layout changed).
- **Not yet**: target poses from animation clips in the sandbox (the API takes any `RagdollPose`, so a game can fill it from `samplePoseTick`; the sandbox sim has no clips), ragdolls grabbing or holding things, skinned drawing of ragdolls (parts are boxes), knockdown from impacts other than pushing.
- **Template**: sample `sim/`/`view/` code is the new-project code (VERSION 0.7.9).

## v0.7.8 — Prefabs (2026-11-02)

### [x] Stage 4: Prefabs (done 2026-11-02)
- **Implemented**: `assets/prefabs/*.prefab` (`@format prefab 1`, entity blocks in the scene format, no environment). `scene::savePrefab`/`loadPrefab`/`placePrefab` (`scene/Scene.h`).
  Editor: [Edit] > Save as Prefab... (also the scene tree context menu) saves the selection roots and their children; roots are stored relative to the first root's x/z (height kept).

## v0.7.7 — AI pathfinding (2026-11-02)

### [x] Stage 4: AI pathfinding (grid) (done 2026-11-02)
- **Engine** `mealkit/sim/NavGrid.h` (header only): `NavBakeSettings` (cell size, agent height/radius, step height, layers per column), `bakeNavGrid(physics, min, max, settings)` walks rays down every cell column (several floors per column; a ray starting inside a solid steps down), keeps floors with agent-height headroom, then drops nodes within the agent radius of walls, drops and the area edge. `findPath` is A* over 8 neighbors (no corner cutting, neighbors within the step height), integer costs 10/14, open list keyed by (cost, node number), `maxExpand` limit; returns `Found`/`Partial` (closest node reached)/`None` and waypoints with straight runs merged. `NavSearch` is reused scratch memory (stamped, not state). `NavGrid::hash` for checks.
- **Not baked in the editor** (the roadmap's plan): the grid is baked at match start from the static scene bodies, see `docs/DECISIONS.md` 2026-11-02. Waypoints are not used.
- **Sandbox**: `World::nav` baked in `reset` right after `loadScene` over the match area. Controllers keep `aiRepath`, `aiPath[16]`, `aiPathCount`, `aiPathNext`; a new AI target asks for a search, `controlPawns` runs at most `aiPathsPerTick` searches per tick in controller order, the AI walks the waypoints and searches again at the end of a cut-short path. Tuning `nav`, `aiPathsPerTick`, `aiPathExpand`, `navDebug` (draws walkable cells near each AI and their paths). Replays regenerated.
- **Checked**: Release build, headless run, a bot capture with `navDebug`.

### [x] Stage 4: Virtual cameras (Cinemachine style) + cutscenes (done 2026-11-01)
- **Scene kind** `virtual_camera` (engine built-in like `point_light`, marker + cosmetic so outside the content hash, editor camera icon). Fields `scene::VirtualCameraFields`: body (`CameraBody` ThirdPerson spring arm, FirstPerson, TopDown, Orbit, Rail, Fixed, Free), priority, active, follow/lookAt (object names; empty follow = the possessed Pawn), height, distance and zoom range, start yaw/pitch and pitch range, orbit speed, fov (0 = player setting), dead zone, damping, spring arm wall margin, blend seconds + easing (`CameraEase` EaseInOut/Linear/Cut), rail (object names in order), free move speed.
- **Engine brain** (new folder `camera/`, after `feedback` in the layer order): `camera::Brain` loads the virtual cameras of a flattened scene, shows the highest-priority active one (ties: later object), and blends position/rotation/fov from the last shown pose over the new camera's blend time. Game code only calls `setActive`/`setPriority`/`playCutscene`. `BrainInput` per frame: look/zoom/free-move input, the default target, player fov, shake impulses (added to the result), named-target lookup and a wall ray callback (view-side query). `setFallback` is the camera used when none is active. `apply` fills `FrameParams`, `flatForward` is the move-input direction.
- **Cutscenes** (`asset/Cutscene.h`): `loadCameraClips` bakes the first glTF camera node (world transform, static parents, yfov) per animation at 30 Hz into MealKit coordinates; `sampleCamera`. Engine table `assets/tables/cutscenes.table` (row `asset::CutsceneRow`: `file`, `clip`, `blockInput`, `blendIn`, `blendOut`, `events` = "seconds feedback row" pairs, [Tables] panel, reread on change). `Brain::playCutscene` plays a row, fires its feedback rows as time passes, and blends back to the game camera at the end or on `stopCutscene`.
- **Sandbox**: `OrbitCamera` → `GameCamera` (brain + cutscenes, the old orbit input). `CameraTuning` keeps sensitivity/zoom step/clip planes and gets `fallback` (the old third-person values), `menu` (Orbit behind the menus, replaces `menuOrbitSpeed`), `introCutscene`, `resultsCamera`. The countdown phase plays `intro` (all peers see the phase on the same tick; only input is blocked, the simulation keeps running) and `Playing` stops it; the Results phase activates the arena's `results` Fixed camera. The spring arm rays the physics world ignoring my Pawn's body. Assets: `models/cutscenes/intro.glb` (`make_sample_models.py make_intro_cutscene`, a 3 s sweep), `tables/cutscenes.table`, feedback row `CutsceneStart`, `arena.scene` object `results`.
- **Checked**: unit tests `camera/brain` (look rotation, follow distance, spring arm pull-in, priority switch with linear blend, fallback first person, shake) and `asset/camera_clip` (baked keys and coordinates of intro.glb, no-camera error, table warnings, event order, input block and end of playback); bot captures during the countdown (cutscene) and play (follow camera); Release and Editor builds.
- **Decision**: cutscene events are written in the cutscene table, like animation events (`docs/DECISIONS.md` 2026-11-01).
- **Editor previews** (edit mode, Esc or right-drag stops): the virtual camera inspector section **Look Through** shows the scene view through that camera (a flattened copy with only it active, so field edits show at once; no follow target in edit mode, so it stands at its placement); **View > Preview Cutscene** lists `cutscenes.table` rows and plays one in the scene view with its feedback events, returning to the editor camera at the end. A label shows the camera name or the cutscene time. Automated: `--select <index> --look-through`, `--cutscene <name>`. Scene tree camera icon.
- **Not yet**: animated fov (needs KHR_animation_pointer), animated parents of the camera node, rail paths from object hierarchies (names only, flattened scenes lose parents).
- **Template**: sample `view/` code is the new-project code (VERSION 0.7.6).

## v0.7.5 — animation state table + animation events (2026-11-01)

### [x] Stage 4: Animation state table + animation events (done 2026-11-01)
- **Engine** (`asset/AnimStates.h`, view side only): engine table `assets/tables/animation.table` (row type `asset::AnimState`, registered with the engine tables so the [Tables] panel edits it). Row id `<set>.<state>`, the first row of a set is the entry state. Columns: `clip` (one clip, or a 1D blend `clip value clip value` sorted by value), `blendParam`, `loop`, `speed`, `blendIn`, `transitions` (`<state> if <cond> [and <cond>]` with `> < >= <= == !=`, `param`/`!param` as on/off, `end` for a finished non-looping clip, or `<state> on <trigger>`; first match wins), `events` (`cycle fraction feedback row` pairs).
  `AnimStates` parses the table (warnings for bad ids, unknown targets, bad conditions) and rereads it when the file changes (`poll`). `AnimParams` holds named values and one-frame triggers. `updateAnim` runs transitions, advances the state's phase (blend states: cycle length = lerp of the two active clips, clips share the normalized phase), cross-fades from the previous state over `blendIn`, and returns event row names crossed this update (the fading-out state fires none). `animPose` samples the result.
- **Feedback**: `feedback::Player::play(row, position)` plays a row by name (the old `preview` calls it), used for animation events.
- **Sandbox**: `PawnLook.idleClip/runClip/runSpeed/blendSeconds` → `animSet` (`pawn`). `drawWorld` sets `speed` and `grounded` per Pawn, `onWorldEvent` gives the pusher a `push` trigger, events go through `ctx.feedback.play` at the Pawn. `assets/tables/animation.table`: `pawn.idle`, `pawn.move` (idle↔run blend by speed, footsteps at 0.25/0.75), `pawn.wave` (on push, back at end). `feedback.table` new row `Footstep` (dust effect).
- **Checked**: unit test `asset/animation_states` (parsing and warnings, AND conditions, trigger only from listed states, `end`, blend sorting, four footsteps in two cycles, cross-fade finishing); sandbox bot capture shows the running blend and footstep dust; Release and Editor builds.
- **Decision**: animation events are written in the table, not read from glTF markers (`docs/DECISIONS.md` 2026-11-01).
- **Not yet**: 2D blend spaces (when a game needs strafing), a dedicated editor preview of states (the [Tables] panel edits the file and a running game picks changes up), animation LOD (optimization candidate 7).
- **Template**: sample `view/` code is the new-project code (VERSION 0.7.5).

## v0.7.4 — character movement modes (2026-11-01)

### [x] Stage 4: Character movement modes (done 2026-11-01)
- **Engine physics** (`physics/PhysicsWorld.h`): kinematic characters = Jolt `CharacterVirtual` (`createCharacter`/`destroyCharacter`/`moveCharacter`, `characterPosition`/`Velocity`/`Ground`). Each one gets a kinematic capsule body of the same size that follows it (`MoveKinematic`), so boxes, other Pawns and rays see it; the character ignores its own body. Characters are in `hashState`, `writeState` (`character <id> p .. v .. ground ..` lines in desync dumps), `save`/`restore` (recreated by id like bodies, `CharacterVirtual::SaveState` bytes) and snapshot bytes (late join). Slope limit is passed as a cosine (callers use `detCos`); Jolt's own `ACos`/`Cos` are deterministic.
- **Engine movement modes** (`sim/Movement.h`, header only): `MoveMode` Kinematic/Capsule/Floating, `MoveTuning` (speed, accelerations, jump, ground probe, slope/step/stick for kinematic, hover gap/spring/damping for floating), `MoveIntent` (wish, jump), `Mover` state (mode, body, character, grounded, ground gap). `createMover`/`destroyMover`, `move()` before the physics step (returns "jumped"), `senseMover()` after it, `moverVelocity`/`setMoverVelocity` (jump pads). Kinematic applies gravity itself and rides ground velocity (moving platforms); Capsule is the old sandbox code; Floating springs toward `floatHeight` over a downward ray.
- **Sandbox**: `Pawn.body`/`grounded` → `Pawn.mover`. Tuning `playerSpeed`, `groundAccel`, `airAccel`, `jumpSpeed`, `jumpMaxRise`, `groundProbe` → nested `move.*` (`move.speed` etc. in `game.tuning`). `bodies.table` new column `movement`: normal = Capsule (AI too), light = Kinematic, heavy = Floating, chosen in the lobby like before.
- **Checked**: unit test `sim/movement_modes` (all modes stand, kinematic climbs a 0.3 m step, snapshot bytes restored into another world give the same per-tick hashes); with the first body row switched to each mode: the three scenarios, headless replay, `--shadow --rewind-test`, `mp_test -Headless -Clients 2 -Ticks 3000 -LateJoin` PASS, screen captures. Replay `arena_4p_seed7.rec` re-recorded (Pawn state layout changed).
- **Not yet**: active ragdoll (own ROADMAP item, done in v0.7.9), crouching/swimming/ladders, kinematic characters pushing each other (they see each other's kinematic bodies as walls), cross-platform CI comparison of kinematic runs (CI replays use capsule bodies).
- **Template**: sample `sim/` code is the new-project code (VERSION 0.7.4); project `CLAUDE.md` mentions movement modes.

## v0.7.3 — game flow framework (2026-11-01)

### [x] Stage 4: Game flow framework (done 2026-11-01)
- **Engine** (`sim/MatchFlow.h`, header only): `MatchFlow` state (`phase` Waiting/Countdown/Playing/RoundEnd/Results, `phaseEnd`, `round`, `roundWinner`, `matchWinner`, `roundWins[side]`), `MatchRules` (minPlayers, countdown, roundLength, roundEndDelay, rounds = best of N, scoreLimit, elimination, teamCount), `MatchPlayer` (present, alive, score; built by the game each tick). `advanceMatch()` returns one `FlowEvent` per tick (CountdownStart, RoundStart, RoundEnd, MatchEnd); `startMatchFlow()` at match start. Side = slot % teamCount, or the slot for free for all. Round end: score limit → elimination (at most one side alive; with one side, none alive) → time up (highest score, tie = draw `kNoSide`). The match ends after a side has more than half of `rounds`, or after `rounds` rounds. Unit test `sim/match_flow`.
- **Sandbox**: `WorldState.flow` replaces `phase`/`phaseEnd` (`MatchPhase` is now an alias of `FlowPhase`, `Finished` → `Results`). Tuning `matchLength` → `roundLength`, new `roundEndDelay` (3 s), `rounds`, `scoreLimit`, `lives`, `teams`, `minPlayers`. `PlayerSlot.score` (+1 to every player of another side when someone falls during play) and `lives` (0 = unlimited; out players don't respawn until the next round). `onRoundStart` clears scores, refills lives and, from round 2, respawns everyone at the spawn points; new tick event `RoundEnd` (value = winning side or -1). Defaults keep the old one-round, 120 s match, now with a 3 s round-end pause before results.
- **Checked**: unit test, the three scenarios (paths now `flow.phase`, `flow.round`), a temporary 3-round/2-lives tuning run headless and with `--shadow`; replay `arena_4p_seed7.rec` re-recorded (simulation results changed).
- **Not yet**: lobby team choice (loadouts), HUD round/score display and a round-result screen (screens still show falls; `flow.round`/`roundWinner` are there to read).
- **Template**: sample `sim/` code is the new-project code (VERSION 0.7.3); project `CLAUDE.md` mentions the match flow.

## v0.7.1 — scenario tests for game code (2026-11-01)

### [x] Stage 4: Scenario tests for game code (done 2026-11-01)
- **Format** (`game/Scenario.h`): `tests/*.scenario` text: `seed`, `scene`, `inputs <rec next to the file>` (else the solo bot), `ticks`, and `tick <n|seconds s> <path> <op> <value>` checks on the state after that tick. Paths are `WorldState` field paths like the [State] panel; `reflect::resolvePath` now also walks live `Pool` slots (`pool[slot]`). `==`/`!=` read the value with the file parser (`reflect::parseValue`, new) and compare bytes; `< <= > >=` compare numbers.
- **Running**: `<game>_headless --test <file>` (and `--headless --test`): the solo offline bot run, or the recording replayed (recorded hash differences are only logged, since rules may change on purpose). `SCENARIO ok <file> checks=N` / `SCENARIO FAIL <file>:<line> ...`, exit code 6. `--update-expected` rewrites the `==` values with the current results (the `record_replay.ps1` counterpart).
- **Editor**: [Save Scenario] in the play bar (solo play keeping replays, not while viewing the past): copies `current.rec` to `tests/scenarioN.rec` and writes `tests/scenarioN.scenario` with one `==` check per top-level scalar state field. Not tried by clicking in the editor yet (built; the same file format is tested from the command line).
- **Checks**: `check.ps1` det group "Scenario tests (samples/*/tests)" and `ci_test.ps1` run every `samples/*/tests/*.scenario`; scenario files are in the determinism key and `samples/*/tests/` forces the group. Unit test `game/scenario`. Sandbox scenarios: `match_flow` (countdown → playing by seconds), `bot_falls` (bot 900 ticks), `arena_4p` (the committed 4-player recording).

## v0.7.0 — stage 3 done (2026-11-01)

Finished the last v0.7-blocking item of stage 3 (crash reports) and bumped to v0.7 (ROADMAP "Versions": v0.n = the engine that finished stage n). Stage 2 (v0.6) had finished while the version stayed 0.5.x, so there is no 0.6.0; the version goes 0.5.4 → 0.7.0.
- **Stage 3 contents** (entries below): saved replays, desync diagnosis, late join/reconnect (v0.5.4), bug report bundles, host migration, EOS backend part 1, crash reports.
- **Passed over since they don't block v0.7**: EOS measurements (two PCs + portal setup), external library review follow-ups, local multiplayer on one PC. They remain in the ROADMAP stage 3 section.
- **Impact**: no template or file format changes; existing projects only get the new engine version from `tools\upgrade_project.ps1`. Docs repo `ENGINE_VERSION` 0.7. Modules made for engine 0.5 (`cooldown`) still build, but `module.ps1` warns until a version for 0.7 is in the registry.

### Stage 3 items (finished between v0.5.4 and v0.7.0)

### [x] Stage 3: Crash reports (done 2026-11-01)
- **At the crash** (`platform/System.h` `installCrashHandler`): Windows `SetUnhandledExceptionFilter` + `abort`/`std::terminate`/pure call/invalid parameter turned into an exception (CRT abort dialog off) → `crash/pending/crash.txt` (exception code, address, thread) + `crash.dmp` (`MiniDumpWithIndirectlyReferencedMemory`, about 300 KB; `dbghelp.dll` loaded at install, no import lib). POSIX: fatal signals write `crash.txt` only. Not installed under a debugger. Then the game hook (`game/BugReport.cpp` `onCrash`) writes `info.txt` (same lines as bug reports + tick/players at the crash, kept current by `updateCrashReport` every frame), `log.txt` and `recording.txt` (path of the input recording, flushed every tick).
- **Next run** (`collectCrashReport`, before `keepReplay` moves `current.rec`): bundles pending/ + the recording into `crash/YYYY-MM-DD_HH-MM.zip`, empties pending/, WARN log, and (not in automated runs) a yes/no dialog `askDialog` that opens the folder.
- **Viewing**: same layout as bug report zips, so the editor's [Open Replay] / `--replay <zip>` plays to the crash tick and pauses; the log and notification say "Crash report".
- **Bug reports**: `info.txt` builder shared (`infoText` + `matchLines`), tick/players/localPlayer lines now come after `tuning`.
- **Switch**: project setting `bugReports` (same as F9); join windows (`playerFiles` off) don't arm it. For tests `--crash-at <frame>` (null write).
- **Verified**: unit test `game crash_report`; `check.ps1` "Crash report" (crash at frame 120 → `crash.dmp` → next run makes one zip, pending emptied); manual: editor `--replay <crash zip>` paused at tick 45.
- **Left**: no upload (DECISIONS deferred table: Crashpad/sentry). No handler in the editor process itself. Stack overflow may fail to write the dump (no `SetThreadStackGuarantee` yet).

### miniaudio CVE-2026-32837 fix (2026-11-01)
